Afraid? Um... I don't know if I'd use the word *afraid*, exactly. I just try to be careful, you know? I want to be a good worker and stay in shape.

I hope I don't sound vain or anything, but... especially when I look at my stomach and my waist, I just feel like... well, if I'm not smaller and tight-looking right there, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. And at my age, you really have to work hard to keep that discipline, right? I just want to make sure I still look like a guy who can pull his own weight and get the job done. That makes sense, doesn't it?

No, no, I don't really do that. I mean... I'm pretty careful about what I eat, like I said. \n\nI guess... sometimes if I've been working a really long shift, I might feel a little extra hungry when I get home, but I definitely don't sit down and eat a whole bunch of food all at once. That's the best way to keep your energy steady without getting weighed down, right? Um... eating quickly? No, I don't really think so. I mean, I usually take my time. 

I guess... well, if I only have a short break at work, you know, sometimes you just have to get something down fast so you can get back to the floor. But even then, I'm just having my usual small things. I wouldn't say I'm rushing or... or scarfing it down or anything like that. 

I actually think it's better to eat slow anyway, right? It helps your digestion and keeps you from feeling sluggish. I always try to be mindful about it. That's the right way to do it, isn't it?

Um... terrified? I mean, I wouldn't use the word *terrified*. That makes it sound like a phobia or something, right? I just... I just like to be careful. 

Like I was saying before, if I have something heavy—like rice, or bread, or if I have to eat something a little greasy on a job site—I just... I get this nagging feeling. You know, like a guy who's ready to work and can stay quick on his feet. That's a good thing in my line of work, right? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid? Um... I don't know if I'd use the word *afraid*, exactly. I just try to be careful, you know? I want to be a good worker and stay in shape. \n\nBut... well, I guess I do get a little worked up about certain things. Like, if I eat rice, or bread, or... maybe something greasy. I just can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. It just feels like it turns straight into fat. So, I'll usually check the scale the next morning, or, you know, see how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nIt's just about having a little discipline and keeping an eye on things, right? I hope that doesn't sound too strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low right now?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? I really don't think it's an issue. I mean... I know the occupational doctor, and, well, my sister too, they keep saying that I'm in danger. Which... I hate to disagree with a doctor, or worry my family, but I really think they're just making it a much bigger deal than it actually is. \n\nThey just don't really understand the physical demands of what I do. I'm just trying to keep myself light, you know? Sharp, and... and ready for work. If I get too heavy or slow down, I can't hustle on the dock like I need to. You understand where I'm coming from, right? I'm just trying to be a good, reliable worker. I'm doing the right thing by staying in shape, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look underweight when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? When I look in the mirror, I don't really see \"underweight.\" I just... I guess I see areas where I need to stay vigilant. \n\nI hope I don't sound vain or anything, but... especially when I look at my stomach and my waist, I just feel like... well, if I'm not smaller and tight-looking right there, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. And at my age, you really have to work hard to keep that discipline, right? I just want to make sure I still look like a guy who can pull his own weight and get the job done. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Um... a large amount of food? No, no, I don't really do that. I mean... I'm pretty careful about what I eat, like I said. \n\nI guess... sometimes if I've been working a really long shift, I might feel a little extra hungry when I get home, but I definitely don't sit down and eat a whole bunch of food all at once. That just wouldn't be very disciplined, you know? And I try really hard to keep my routine strict so I don't lose my edge. \n\nI just stick to my regular, small portions. That's the best way to keep your energy steady without getting weighed down, right? I'm doing the right thing by keeping it controlled like that, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? I haven't really been tracking it too closely. I mean, I know I've slimmed down some, but I wouldn't call it... you know, \"significant\" or anything alarming. \n\nI guess my work pants are a little looser than they used to be, but that's just a sign that my routine is working, right? Just shedding a little extra bulk so I can move faster on the dock. I haven't been on a scale in a while, so I couldn't give you an exact number, but... I feel like it's just a normal amount for a guy trying to stay fit for his job. \n\nIt's better to be on the lighter side when you're doing heavy lifting all day, isn't it? I'm just trying to be smart about my health, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Um... lost control? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm pretty good at keeping things in check. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes if I'm really exhausted after a double shift, I might feel a little... scattered, you know? Like I'm just eating whatever's quickest before I crash. But I wouldn't call it losing control. I always know exactly what I'm having and I keep it to my usual amounts. \n\nI just think it's important to stay disciplined, especially at my age. You can't just let yourself go, right? I mean, keeping a tight rein on things is just part of being responsible, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are eating much more quickly than usual during a meal?\n\nPatient: Um... eating quickly? I mean, I usually take my time. \n\nI guess... well, if I only have a short break at work, you know, sometimes you just have to get something down fast so you can get back to the floor. It helps your digestion and keeps you from feeling sluggish. I always try to be mindful about it. That's the right way to do it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining a small amount of weight would be bad for you?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess \"bad\" might be a strong word, but... I do worry about it, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nIt's just... like, if I end up eating some rice, or a piece of bread, or maybe something a little greasy, I just... I can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. Like it's just going to turn into pure bulk overnight. So... I'll usually check the scale the next morning, or just see how my belt fits, just to make absolutely sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nI just want to stay sharp and light on my feet for work, you know? Well, like I was just mentioning a minute ago... I guess "anxious" might be a fair way to put it.

I'm just trying to keep myself light, you know? Sharp, and... and ready for work. If I get too heavy or slow down, I can't hustle on the dock like I need to. And at my age, you really have to work hard to keep that discipline, right? I just want to make sure I still look like a guy who can pull his own weight and get the job done. No, no, I don't really do that. I mean... That just wouldn't be very disciplined, you know? And I try really hard to keep my routine strict so I don't lose my edge. \n\nI just stick to my regular, small portions. That's the best way to keep your energy steady without getting weighed down, right? I'm doing the right thing by keeping it controlled like that, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? I haven't really been tracking it too closely. Just shedding a little extra bulk so I can move faster on the dock. I haven't been on a scale in a while, so I couldn't give you an exact number, but... I feel like it's just a normal amount for a guy trying to stay fit for his job. \n\nIt's better to be on the lighter side when you're doing heavy lifting all day, isn't it? I'm just trying to be smart about my health, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Um... lost control? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm pretty good at keeping things in check. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes if I'm really exhausted after a double shift, I might feel a little... scattered, you know? Like I'm just eating whatever's quickest before I crash. But I wouldn't call it losing control. I always know exactly what I'm having and I keep it to my usual amounts. \n\nI just think it's important to stay disciplined, especially at my age. You can't just let yourself go, right? I mean, keeping a tight rein on things is just part of being responsible, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are eating much more quickly than usual during a meal?\n\nPatient: Um... eating quickly? No, I don't really think so. I mean, I usually take my time. \n\nI guess... well, if I only have a short break at work, you know, sometimes you just have to get something down fast so you can get back to the floor. I do worry about it, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nIt's just... like, if I end up eating some rice, or a piece of bread, or maybe something a little greasy, I just... I can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. Like it's just going to turn into pure bulk overnight. So... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... I wouldn't really say it's about vanity or anything like that. I mean, I'm a guy in my fifties, I'm not trying to win any beauty contests or anything, you know? 

But... I guess if I'm being completely honest... when I look in the mirror, especially at my stomach and my waist... if I don't look, um, smaller and tight-looking, it just... it really bothers me. It makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. Like, if I'm not tight around the middle, then I'm just not the disciplined man I've always been on the warehouse floor. 

I've just always prided myself on keeping in line and working hard, and I guess I just need to see that discipline when I look in the mirror. That's a pretty normal way for a guy to feel about himself, isn't it? Just wanting to look like you're still a hard worker?

No, no, I don't really do that. I mean... I'm pretty careful about what I eat, like I said. \n\nI guess... sometimes if I've been working a really long shift, I might feel a little extra hungry when I get home, but I definitely don't sit down and eat a whole bunch of food all at once. That just wouldn't be very disciplined, you know? And I try really hard to keep my routine strict so I don't lose my edge. \n\nI just stick to my regular, small portions. That's the best way to keep your energy steady without getting weighed down, right? Just shedding a little extra bulk so I can move faster on the dock. I haven't been on a scale in a while, so I couldn't give you an exact number, but... I feel like it's just a normal amount for a guy trying to stay fit for his job. \n\nIt's better to be on the lighter side when you're doing heavy lifting all day, isn't it? No, I don't really think so. I mean, I usually take my time. \n\nI guess... well, if I only have a short break at work, you know, sometimes you just have to get something down fast so you can get back to the floor. But even then, I'm just having my usual small things. I wouldn't say I'm rushing or... or scarfing it down or anything like that. \n\nI actually think it's better to eat slow anyway, right? It helps your digestion and keeps you from feeling sluggish. I always try to be mindful about it. That's the right way to do it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining a small amount of weight would be bad for you?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess \"bad\" might be a strong word, but... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I don't know if "unattractive" is the right word. I mean, I'm just a regular working guy, right? I'm not trying to be a model. 

But... like I was just saying about the mirror... if I gained weight, even just a little bit, and I lost that tightness around my waist and my stomach... I think I'd just look... soft. You know? Like I let myself go. And if I look soft, then I don't look like that disciplined, hard-working guy I've always been on the warehouse floor. I'd just look like someone who gave up. 

I just want to look like a guy who still has his edge and takes his job seriously. That's a good thing to want, right? To look like you still take pride in yourself?

That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Um... a large amount of food? No, no, I don't really do that. I mean... That's the best way to keep your energy steady without getting weighed down, right? I'm doing the right thing by keeping it controlled like that, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? I haven't really been tracking it too closely. Just shedding a little extra bulk so I can move faster on the dock. I haven't been on a scale in a while, so I couldn't give you an exact number, but... I feel like it's just a normal amount for a guy trying to stay fit for his job. \n\nIt's better to be on the lighter side when you're doing heavy lifting all day, isn't it? I'm just trying to be smart about my health, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Um... lost control? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm pretty good at keeping things in check. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes if I'm really exhausted after a double shift, I might feel a little... scattered, you know? Like I'm just eating whatever's quickest before I crash. But I wouldn't call it losing control. I always know exactly what I'm having and I keep it to my usual amounts. \n\nI just think it's important to stay disciplined, especially at my age. You can't just let yourself go, right? Well, "fear" is a pretty strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't say I'm terrified or anything like that. I'm just... careful. 

But, I mean, like I mentioned before, if I eat something heavy—like rice, or bread, or if something's a bit greasy—I just... I can't stop thinking about it. It feels like it's just going to sit on me right away, you know? Like it's instantly turning into softness.

That just wouldn't be very disciplined, you know? And I try really hard to keep my routine strict so I don't lose my edge. \n\nI just stick to my regular, small portions. That's the best way to keep your energy steady without getting weighed down, right? I'm doing the right thing by keeping it controlled like that, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? I haven't really been tracking it too closely. I mean, I know I've slimmed down some, but I wouldn't call it... you know, \"significant\" or anything alarming. \n\nI guess my work pants are a little looser than they used to be, but that's just a sign that my routine is working, right? Just shedding a little extra bulk so I can move faster on the dock. I haven't been on a scale in a while, so I couldn't give you an exact number, but... I feel like it's just a normal amount for a guy trying to stay fit for his job. \n\nIt's better to be on the lighter side when you're doing heavy lifting all day, isn't it? I'm just trying to be smart about my health, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Um... lost control? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm pretty good at keeping things in check. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes if I'm really exhausted after a double shift, I might feel a little... scattered, you know? Like I'm just eating whatever's quickest before I crash. But I wouldn't call it losing control. I always know exactly what I'm having and I keep it to my usual amounts. \n\nI just think it's important to stay disciplined, especially at my age. You can't just let yourself go, right? I mean, keeping a tight rein on things is just part of being responsible, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are eating much more quickly than usual during a meal?\n\nPatient: Um... eating quickly? No, I don't really think so. I mean, I usually take my time. \n\nI guess... well, if I only have a short break at work, you know, sometimes you just have to get something down fast so you can get back to the floor. But even then, I'm just having my usual small things. I wouldn't say I'm rushing or... or scarfing it down or anything like that. \n\nI actually think it's better to eat slow anyway, right? It helps your digestion and keeps you from feeling sluggish. I always try to be mindful about it. That's the right way to do it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining a small amount of weight would be bad for you?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess \"bad\" might be a strong word, but... I do worry about it, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nIt's just... like, if I end up eating some rice, or a piece of bread, or maybe something a little greasy, I just... I can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. Like it's just going to turn into pure bulk overnight. So... I'll usually check the scale the next morning, or just see how my belt fits, just to make absolutely sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nI just want to stay sharp and light on my feet for work, you know? It's good to keep a close eye on those things so they don't get out of hand... right?\n\nDoctor: Are you terrified of gaining any weight at all?\n\nPatient: Um... terrified? I mean, I wouldn't use the word *terrified*. That makes it sound like a phobia or something, right? I just... I just like to be careful. \n\nLike I was saying before, if I have something heavy—like rice, or bread, or if I have to eat something a little greasy on a job site—I just... I get this nagging feeling. Like I can't stop thinking about how it's just going to sit right on me, you know? Like it's instantly going to turn into fat. So, yeah, I'll check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt feels, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nBut it's not terror, it's just... being proactive. It's better to catch it early before it becomes a real problem, right? That's just being responsible about your health, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just mentioning a minute ago... I guess \"anxious\" might be a fair way to put it. \n\nIf I end up having something heavy, like that bread or rice we were talking about, my mind just kind of loops on it. I just keep picturing it sitting right there on my stomach, weighing me down and making me soft. So, yeah, maybe that is a little bit of anxiety? \n\nBut that's exactly why I step on the scale or check my belt notch the next morning. It just... it settles my nerves. Once I see everything is still the same, the anxiety goes away, and I can get to the warehouse and do my job without stressing over it. That makes sense, doesn't it? It makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. Like, if I'm not tight around the middle, then I'm just not the disciplined man I've always been on the warehouse floor. \n\nI've just always prided myself on keeping in line and working hard, and I guess I just need to see that discipline when I look in the mirror. I think I'd just look... soft. You know? Like I let myself go. And if I look soft, then I don't look like that disciplined, hard-working guy I've always been on the warehouse floor. I'd just look like someone who gave up. \n\nI just want to look like a guy who still has his edge and takes his job seriously. That's a good thing to want, right? To look like you still take pride in yourself?\n\nDoctor: Do you fear gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, \"fear\" is a pretty strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't say I'm terrified or anything like that. Larger than I normally would? Um... no, not really. I'm... I'm a creature of habit, I guess. 

I stick to my regular portions pretty strictly. I mean, if I stray from what I usually have, I just start to feel... off, I guess. So I keep it very consistent. I know exactly what I'm going to eat and when, and I don't really deviate from that. 

I just think it's better to keep things predictable, you know? That way there are no surprises and I don't have to worry about feeling heavy or sluggish the next day at work. That's a good habit to have at my age, right? Just keeping everything steady and disciplined?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, "disaster" sounds a bit extreme, doesn't it? I wouldn't call it a disaster. I just... I like to keep things under control. 

Like I was saying earlier, if I have something like bread or rice, or anything a bit greasy, it just... it plays on my mind. I can't stop thinking that it's going to instantly turn into fat, just sitting right there on my stomach. 

So, I guess I'd say I'd be pretty upset if I gained weight, yeah. That's why I always check the scale the next morning, or see how my belt fits. I just need to be sure I didn't let myself go. It's just a way of staying on top of things, you know? Keeping myself in check so I can do my job properly. That makes sense, doesn't it? Just wanting to stay fit and disciplined?

When I look in the mirror, I don't really see \"underweight.\" I just... I guess I see areas where I need to stay vigilant. \n\nI hope I don't sound vain or anything, but... especially when I look at my stomach and my waist, I just feel like... well, if I'm not smaller and tight-looking right there, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. And at my age, you really have to work hard to keep that discipline, right? I just want to make sure I still look like a guy who can pull his own weight and get the job done. That makes sense, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Um... a large amount of food? No, no, I don't really do that. I mean... I'm pretty careful about what I eat, like I said. \n\nI guess... sometimes if I've been working a really long shift, I might feel a little extra hungry when I get home, but I definitely don't sit down and eat a whole bunch of food all at once. That just wouldn't be very disciplined, you know? And I try really hard to keep my routine strict so I don't lose my edge. \n\nI just stick to my regular, small portions. That's the best way to keep your energy steady without getting weighed down, right? I'm doing the right thing by keeping it controlled like that, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? I haven't really been tracking it too closely. I mean, I know I've slimmed down some, but I wouldn't call it... you know, \"significant\" or anything alarming. \n\nI guess my work pants are a little looser than they used to be, but that's just a sign that my routine is working, right? Just shedding a little extra bulk so I can move faster on the dock. I haven't been on a scale in a while, so I couldn't give you an exact number, but... I feel like it's just a normal amount for a guy trying to stay fit for his job. \n\nIt's better to be on the lighter side when you're doing heavy lifting all day, isn't it? I'm just trying to be smart about my health, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Um... lost control? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm pretty good at keeping things in check. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes if I'm really exhausted after a double shift, I might feel a little... scattered, you know? Like I'm just eating whatever's quickest before I crash. But I wouldn't call it losing control. I always know exactly what I'm having and I keep it to my usual amounts. \n\nI just think it's important to stay disciplined, especially at my age. You can't just let yourself go, right? I mean, keeping a tight rein on things is just part of being responsible, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are eating much more quickly than usual during a meal?\n\nPatient: Um... eating quickly? No, I don't really think so. I mean, I usually take my time. \n\nI guess... well, if I only have a short break at work, you know, sometimes you just have to get something down fast so you can get back to the floor. But even then, I'm just having my usual small things. I wouldn't say I'm rushing or... or scarfing it down or anything like that. \n\nI actually think it's better to eat slow anyway, right? It helps your digestion and keeps you from feeling sluggish. I always try to be mindful about it. That's the right way to do it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining a small amount of weight would be bad for you?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess \"bad\" might be a strong word, but... I do worry about it, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nIt's just... like, if I end up eating some rice, or a piece of bread, or maybe something a little greasy, I just... I can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. Like it's just going to turn into pure bulk overnight. So... I'll usually check the scale the next morning, or just see how my belt fits, just to make absolutely sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nI just want to stay sharp and light on my feet for work, you know? It's good to keep a close eye on those things so they don't get out of hand... right?\n\nDoctor: Are you terrified of gaining any weight at all?\n\nPatient: Um... terrified? I mean, I wouldn't use the word *terrified*. That makes it sound like a phobia or something, right? I just... I just like to be careful. \n\nLike I was saying before, if I have something heavy—like rice, or bread, or if I have to eat something a little greasy on a job site—I just... I get this nagging feeling. Like I can't stop thinking about how it's just going to sit right on me, you know? Like it's instantly going to turn into fat. So, yeah, I'll check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt feels, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nBut it's not terror, it's just... being proactive. It's better to catch it early before it becomes a real problem, right? That's just being responsible about your health, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just mentioning a minute ago... I guess \"anxious\" might be a fair way to put it. \n\nIf I end up having something heavy, like that bread or rice we were talking about, my mind just kind of loops on it. I just keep picturing it sitting right there on my stomach, weighing me down and making me soft. So, yeah, maybe that is a little bit of anxiety? \n\nBut that's exactly why I step on the scale or check my belt notch the next morning. It just... it settles my nerves. Once I see everything is still the same, the anxiety goes away, and I can get to the warehouse and do my job without stressing over it. That makes sense, doesn't it? Just wanting to put your mind at ease so you can focus on your day?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel unhappy about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't really say it's about vanity or anything like that. I mean, I'm a guy in my fifties, I'm not trying to win any beauty contests or anything, you know? \n\nBut... I guess if I'm being completely honest... when I look in the mirror, especially at my stomach and my waist... if I don't look, um, smaller and tight-looking, it just... it really bothers me. It makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. Like, if I'm not tight around the middle, then I'm just not the disciplined man I've always been on the warehouse floor. \n\nI've just always prided myself on keeping in line and working hard, and I guess I just need to see that discipline when I look in the mirror. I think I'd just look... soft. You know? Like I let myself go. And if I look soft, then I don't look like that disciplined, hard-working guy I've always been on the warehouse floor. Just shedding a little extra bulk so I can move faster on the dock. I haven't been on a scale in a while, so I couldn't give you an exact number, but... I feel like it's just a normal amount for a guy trying to stay fit for his job. \n\nIt's better to be on the lighter side when you're doing heavy lifting all day, isn't it? I'm just trying to be smart about my health, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Um... lost control? No, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm pretty good at keeping things in check. \n\nI guess... well, sometimes if I'm really exhausted after a double shift, I might feel a little... scattered, you know? Like I'm just eating whatever's quickest before I crash. But I wouldn't call it losing control. I always know exactly what I'm having and I keep it to my usual amounts. \n\nI just think it's important to stay disciplined, especially at my age. You can't just let yourself go, right? I mean, keeping a tight rein on things is just part of being responsible, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you are eating much more quickly than usual during a meal?\n\nPatient: Um... eating quickly? No, I don't really think so. I mean, I usually take my time. \n\nI guess... well, if I only have a short break at work, you know, sometimes you just have to get something down fast so you can get back to the floor. But even then, I'm just having my usual small things. I wouldn't say I'm rushing or... or scarfing it down or anything like that. \n\nI actually think it's better to eat slow anyway, right? It helps your digestion and keeps you from feeling sluggish. I always try to be mindful about it. That's the right way to do it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining a small amount of weight would be bad for you?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess \"bad\" might be a strong word, but... I do worry about it, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nIt's just... like, if I end up eating some rice, or a piece of bread, or maybe something a little greasy, I just... I can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. Like it's just going to turn into pure bulk overnight. So... I'll usually check the scale the next morning, or just see how my belt fits, just to make absolutely sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nI just want to stay sharp and light on my feet for work, you know? It's good to keep a close eye on those things so they don't get out of hand... right?\n\nDoctor: Are you terrified of gaining any weight at all?\n\nPatient: Um... terrified? I mean, I wouldn't use the word *terrified*. That makes it sound like a phobia or something, right? I just... I get this nagging feeling. Like I can't stop thinking about how it's just going to sit right on me, you know? Like it's instantly going to turn into fat. So, yeah, I'll check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt feels, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nBut it's not terror, it's just... being proactive. It's better to catch it early before it becomes a real problem, right? That's just being responsible about your health, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just mentioning a minute ago... I guess \"anxious\" might be a fair way to put it. \n\nIf I end up having something heavy, like that bread or rice we were talking about, my mind just kind of loops on it. I just keep picturing it sitting right there on my stomach, weighing me down and making me soft. So, yeah, maybe that is a little bit of anxiety? \n\nBut that's exactly why I step on the scale or check my belt notch the next morning. It just... it settles my nerves. Once I see everything is still the same, the anxiety goes away, and I can get to the warehouse and do my job without stressing over it. That makes sense, doesn't it? Just wanting to put your mind at ease so you can focus on your day?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel unhappy about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Well... It makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. I'll usually check the scale the next morning, or just see how my belt fits, just to make absolutely sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nI just want to stay sharp and light on my feet for work, you know? It's good to keep a close eye on those things so they don't get out of hand... right?\n\nDoctor: Are you terrified of gaining any weight at all?\n\nPatient: Um... terrified? I mean, I wouldn't use the word *terrified*. That makes it sound like a phobia or something, right? I just... I just like to be careful. \n\nLike I was saying before, if I have something heavy—like rice, or bread, or if I have to eat something a little greasy on a job site—I just... I get this nagging feeling. Like I can't stop thinking about how it's just going to sit right on me, you know? Like it's instantly going to turn into fat. So, yeah, I'll check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt feels, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nBut it's not terror, it's just... being proactive. It's better to catch it early before it becomes a real problem, right? That's just being responsible about your health, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just mentioning a minute ago... I guess \"anxious\" might be a fair way to put it. \n\nIf I end up having something heavy, like that bread or rice we were talking about, my mind just kind of loops on it. I just keep picturing it sitting right there on my stomach, weighing me down and making me soft. So, yeah, maybe that is a little bit of anxiety? \n\nBut that's exactly why I step on the scale or check my belt notch the next morning. It just... it settles my nerves. Once I see everything is still the same, the anxiety goes away, and I can get to the warehouse and do my job without stressing over it. That makes sense, doesn't it? Just wanting to put your mind at ease so you can focus on your day?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel unhappy about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't really say it's about vanity or anything like that. I mean, I'm a guy in my fifties, I'm not trying to win any beauty contests or anything, you know? \n\nBut... I guess if I'm being completely honest... when I look in the mirror, especially at my stomach and my waist... if I don't look, um, smaller and tight-looking, it just... it really bothers me. It makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. Like, if I'm not tight around the middle, then I'm just not the disciplined man I've always been on the warehouse floor. \n\nI've just always prided myself on keeping in line and working hard, and I guess I just need to see that discipline when I look in the mirror. That's a pretty normal way for a guy to feel about himself, isn't it? Just wanting to look like you're still a hard worker?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look unattractive if you gained a small amount of weight?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if \"unattractive\" is the right word. I mean, I'm just a regular working guy, right? I'm not trying to be a model. \n\nBut... like I was just saying about the mirror... if I gained weight, even just a little bit, and I lost that tightness around my waist and my stomach... I think I'd just look... soft. You know? Like I let myself go. And if I look soft, then I don't look like that disciplined, hard-working guy I've always been on the warehouse floor. I'd just look like someone who gave up. \n\nI just want to look like a guy who still has his edge and takes his job seriously. That's a good thing to want, right? It feels like it's just going to sit on me right away, you know? Like it's instantly turning into softness. \n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do worry about it. That's why I always check the scale or my belt notch the very next morning. I just need to make sure I didn't let myself go after eating something like that. Just making sure you don't lose control?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that is much larger than most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I definitely don't do that. \n\nI mean, sometimes if I eat, say, a whole sandwich or something, it *feels* like a lot to me... but I know it's not really a huge amount compared to what the other guys at the warehouse pack in their coolers. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Excessive? Um... no, not at all. I know we just sort of touched on this, but I really don't ever cross that line into eating too much.

Like, if I eat rice, or bread, or... maybe something greasy. I just can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. It just feels like it turns straight into fat. That's the best way to keep your energy steady without getting weighed down, right? I'm doing the right thing by keeping it controlled like that, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly? I'm just trying to be smart about my health, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Um... lost control? No, no, I wouldn't say that. No, I don't really think so. I mean, I usually take my time. \n\nI guess... well, if I only have a short break at work, you know, sometimes you just have to get something down fast so you can get back to the floor. But even then, I'm just having my usual small things. I wouldn't say I'm rushing or... or scarfing it down or anything like that. \n\nI actually think it's better to eat slow anyway, right? It helps your digestion and keeps you from feeling sluggish. I always try to be mindful about it. That's the right way to do it, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining a small amount of weight would be bad for you?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess \"bad\" might be a strong word, but... I do worry about it, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nIt's just... like, if I end up eating some rice, or a piece of bread, or maybe something a little greasy, I just... I can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. Like it's just going to turn into pure bulk overnight. So... I'll usually check the scale the next morning, or just see how my belt fits, just to make absolutely sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nI just want to stay sharp and light on my feet for work, you know? I just like to be careful. \n\nLike I was saying before, if I have something heavy—like rice, or bread, or if I have to eat something a little greasy on a job site—I just... I get this nagging feeling. Like I can't stop thinking about how it's just going to sit right on me, you know? Like it's instantly going to turn into fat. So, yeah, I'll check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt feels, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nBut it's not terror, it's just... being proactive. It's better to catch it early before it becomes a real problem, right? That's just being responsible about your health, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just mentioning a minute ago... I guess \"anxious\" might be a fair way to put it. \n\nIf I end up having something heavy, like that bread or rice we were talking about, my mind just kind of loops on it. I just keep picturing it sitting right there on my stomach, weighing me down and making me soft. Once I see everything is still the same, the anxiety goes away, and I can get to the warehouse and do my job without stressing over it. That makes sense, doesn't it? Just wanting to put your mind at ease so you can focus on your day?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel unhappy about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Well... It makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. Like, if I'm not tight around the middle, then I'm just not the disciplined man I've always been on the warehouse floor. \n\nI've just always prided myself on keeping in line and working hard, and I guess I just need to see that discipline when I look in the mirror. That's a pretty normal way for a guy to feel about himself, isn't it? Just wanting to look like you're still a hard worker?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look unattractive if you gained a small amount of weight?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if \"unattractive\" is the right word. I mean, I'm just a regular working guy, right? I'm not trying to be a model. \n\nBut... like I was just saying about the mirror... if I gained weight, even just a little bit, and I lost that tightness around my waist and my stomach... I think I'd just look... soft. You know? Like I let myself go. And if I look soft, then I don't look like that disciplined, hard-working guy I've always been on the warehouse floor. I do worry about it, if I'm being completely honest. \n\nIt's just... like, if I end up eating some rice, or a piece of bread, or maybe something a little greasy, I just... I can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. Like it's just going to turn into pure bulk overnight. So... I'll usually check the scale the next morning, or just see how my belt fits, just to make absolutely sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nI just want to stay sharp and light on my feet for work, you know? It's good to keep a close eye on those things so they don't get out of hand... right?\n\nDoctor: Are you terrified of gaining any weight at all?\n\nPatient: Um... terrified? I mean, I wouldn't use the word *terrified*. That makes it sound like a phobia or something, right? I just... I just like to be careful. \n\nLike I was saying before, if I have something heavy—like rice, or bread, or if I have to eat something a little greasy on a job site—I just... I get this nagging feeling. Like I can't stop thinking about how it's just going to sit right on me, you know? Like it's instantly going to turn into fat. So, yeah, I'll check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt feels, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nBut it's not terror, it's just... being proactive. It's better to catch it early before it becomes a real problem, right? That's just being responsible about your health, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just mentioning a minute ago... I guess \"anxious\" might be a fair way to put it. \n\nIf I end up having something heavy, like that bread or rice we were talking about, my mind just kind of loops on it. I just keep picturing it sitting right there on my stomach, weighing me down and making me soft. So, yeah, maybe that is a little bit of anxiety? \n\nBut that's exactly why I step on the scale or check my belt notch the next morning. It just... it settles my nerves. Once I see everything is still the same, the anxiety goes away, and I can get to the warehouse and do my job without stressing over it. That makes sense, doesn't it? Just wanting to put your mind at ease so you can focus on your day?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel unhappy about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't really say it's about vanity or anything like that. I mean, I'm a guy in my fifties, I'm not trying to win any beauty contests or anything, you know? \n\nBut... I guess if I'm being completely honest... when I look in the mirror, especially at my stomach and my waist... if I don't look, um, smaller and tight-looking, it just... it really bothers me. It makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. Like, if I'm not tight around the middle, then I'm just not the disciplined man I've always been on the warehouse floor. \n\nI've just always prided myself on keeping in line and working hard, and I guess I just need to see that discipline when I look in the mirror. That's a pretty normal way for a guy to feel about himself, isn't it? Just wanting to look like you're still a hard worker?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look unattractive if you gained a small amount of weight?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if \"unattractive\" is the right word. I mean, I'm just a regular working guy, right? I'm not trying to be a model. \n\nBut... like I was just saying about the mirror... if I gained weight, even just a little bit, and I lost that tightness around my waist and my stomach... I think I'd just look... soft. You know? Like I let myself go. And if I look soft, then I don't look like that disciplined, hard-working guy I've always been on the warehouse floor. I'd just look like someone who gave up. \n\nI just want to look like a guy who still has his edge and takes his job seriously. That's a good thing to want, right? To look like you still take pride in yourself?\n\nDoctor: Do you fear gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, \"fear\" is a pretty strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't say I'm terrified or anything like that. I'm just... careful. \n\nBut, I mean, like I mentioned before, if I eat something heavy—like rice, or bread, or if something's a bit greasy—I just... I can't stop thinking about it. It feels like it's just going to sit on me right away, you know? Like it's instantly turning into softness. \n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do worry about it. Just making sure you don't lose control?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that is much larger than most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I definitely don't do that. \n\nI mean, sometimes if I eat, say, a whole sandwich or something, it *feels* like a lot to me... but I know it's not really a huge amount compared to what the other guys at the warehouse pack in their coolers. I don't ever just... lose control and eat piles of food, if that's what you mean. \n\nI keep a pretty tight lid on my portions. Like I was saying, I just don't want to get soft, so I'm really careful not to overdo it. I like to know exactly what I'm taking in. It's better to just eat small amounts anyway, right? So, yeah, I'll check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt feels, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. \n\nBut it's not terror, it's just... being proactive. It's better to catch it early before it becomes a real problem, right? That's just being responsible about your health, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel anxious?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just mentioning a minute ago... I guess \"anxious\" might be a fair way to put it. \n\nIf I end up having something heavy, like that bread or rice we were talking about, my mind just kind of loops on it. I just keep picturing it sitting right there on my stomach, weighing me down and making me soft. So, yeah, maybe that is a little bit of anxiety? \n\nBut that's exactly why I step on the scale or check my belt notch the next morning. It just... it settles my nerves. Once I see everything is still the same, the anxiety goes away, and I can get to the warehouse and do my job without stressing over it. That makes sense, doesn't it? Just wanting to put your mind at ease so you can focus on your day?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would make you feel unhappy about your appearance?\n\nPatient: Well... I wouldn't really say it's about vanity or anything like that. I mean, I'm a guy in my fifties, I'm not trying to win any beauty contests or anything, you know? \n\nBut... I guess if I'm being completely honest... when I look in the mirror, especially at my stomach and my waist... if I don't look, um, smaller and tight-looking, it just... it really bothers me. It makes me feel like I'm losing my edge. Like, if I'm not tight around the middle, then I'm just not the disciplined man I've always been on the warehouse floor. \n\nI've just always prided myself on keeping in line and working hard, and I guess I just need to see that discipline when I look in the mirror. That's a pretty normal way for a guy to feel about himself, isn't it? Just wanting to look like you're still a hard worker?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would look unattractive if you gained a small amount of weight?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if \"unattractive\" is the right word. I mean, I'm just a regular working guy, right? I'm not trying to be a model. \n\nBut... like I was just saying about the mirror... if I gained weight, even just a little bit, and I lost that tightness around my waist and my stomach... I think I'd just look... soft. You know? Like I let myself go. And if I look soft, then I don't look like that disciplined, hard-working guy I've always been on the warehouse floor. I'd just look like someone who gave up. \n\nI just want to look like a guy who still has his edge and takes his job seriously. That's a good thing to want, right? To look like you still take pride in yourself?\n\nDoctor: Do you fear gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, \"fear\" is a pretty strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't say I'm terrified or anything like that. I'm just... careful. \n\nBut, I mean, like I mentioned before, if I eat something heavy—like rice, or bread, or if something's a bit greasy—I just... I can't stop thinking about it. It feels like it's just going to sit on me right away, you know? Like it's instantly turning into softness. \n\nSo, yeah, I guess I do worry about it. That's why I always check the scale or my belt notch the very next morning. I just need to make sure I didn't let myself go after eating something like that. It's just a way of keeping myself accountable, making sure I stay disciplined. That's a pretty normal way to handle it, right? Just making sure you don't lose control?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that is much larger than most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, no... no, I definitely don't do that. \n\nI mean, sometimes if I eat, say, a whole sandwich or something, it *feels* like a lot to me... but I know it's not really a huge amount compared to what the other guys at the warehouse pack in their coolers. I don't ever just... lose control and eat piles of food, if that's what you mean. \n\nI keep a pretty tight lid on my portions. Like I was saying, I just don't want to get soft, so I'm really careful not to overdo it. I like to know exactly what I'm taking in. It's better to just eat small amounts anyway, right? It keeps you from getting bogged down and sluggish while you're working?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that is much larger than you normally would in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Larger than I normally would? Um... no, not really. I'm... I'm a creature of habit, I guess. \n\nI stick to my regular portions pretty strictly. I mean, if I stray from what I usually have, I just start to feel... off, I guess. So I keep it very consistent. I know exactly what I'm going to eat and when, and I don't really deviate from that. \n\nI just think it's better to keep things predictable, you know? That way there are no surprises and I don't have to worry about feeling heavy or sluggish the next day at work. That's a good habit to have at my age, right? Just keeping everything steady and disciplined?\n\nDoctor: Do you think gaining weight would be a disaster for you?\n\nPatient: Well, \"disaster\" sounds a bit extreme, doesn't it? I wouldn't call it a disaster. I just... I like to keep things under control. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, if I have something like bread or rice, or anything a bit greasy, it just... it plays on my mind. I can't stop thinking that it's going to instantly turn into fat, just sitting right there on my stomach. \n\nSo, I guess I'd say I'd be pretty upset if I gained weight, yeah. That's why I always check the scale the next morning, or see how my belt fits. I just need to be sure I didn't let myself go. It's just a way of staying on top of things, you know? Keeping myself in check so I can do my job properly. That makes sense, doesn't it? Just wanting to stay fit and disciplined?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that is much larger than you normally would in a very short period of time, and during that time, you felt like you couldn't stop eating?\n\nPatient: No, no, I've never had anything like that happen. I mean, losing control like that... that's just not me. \n\nI'm very disciplined with my meals. I don't ever get to a point where I feel like I can't stop. Honestly, I don't even let myself get that hungry in the first place, so there's no urge to just... gorge myself, you know? \n\nI just portion out exactly what I need to keep going, and once it's gone, it's gone. I don't go back for seconds or anything. It's just a matter of willpower, really. I think it's important to have that kind of self-control, don't you? It shows you respect your body and aren't just giving in to every little craving?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten an amount of food that felt excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Excessive? Um... no, not at all. I know we just sort of touched on this, but I really don't ever cross that line into eating too much. \n\nI just... I keep everything very measured. Working in freight, you know, being on my feet and lifting all day, I can't afford to be weighed down by a heavy meal. So I just stick to my small amounts. I've never looked at what I ate and thought it was excessive, because I always make sure it's the bare minimum of what I need to get by. \n\nIt's just being responsible with my body, really. That's the right way to handle things, isn't it? Just keeping it all strictly in moderation?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you couldn't stop eating once you started?\n\nPatient: No, never.
